# History and Background
At low temperatures, the restivity of normal  metals reaches a cosntant, of which depends on the concentration of impurites, shown in fig 4.1 in 1911, Heiki Kamerlingh-Onnes, had liquidied helium and when measuring the rsitance of a mercury sample noticed a drop in resitances at 4.2K. The resitance was below what he could measure. He named this the **superconductor**
# Excalidraw Data

## Text Elements
Two main types of superconductor which are best understood by looking at the magnetization curve M(H)
Type I and Type II Superconductors hh ^FURbYdsf

Type 1 Superconductors 
To a few percent  the criticaal feild has the following temperature dependance 

 ^F4IgMo4Y

Interfaces 
At any interface the wavefunction must be continuous


  ^jn06ucAD

on the other hand, energy associated as the field does penetrate the superconductor At the interface, the field penetrates by distance   ^WNcerTVj

Taking these two terms together we can calculate the total energy cost of an interafce: 
 ^FtOm56zK
